## R-139 · S-parameter definitions and passivity

*Gathered by research subagents (Course 07 facts files: c07 entry F-0732; c0710 entries F-0825 to F-0828 and F-0843 to F-0845); documents opened.*

- **Keysight, *S-Parameter Design*, Application Note 154, 5952-1087 (published 23 Jul 2020)** [Established]: "Moving to higher and higher frequencies, some problems arise: 1. Equipment is not readily available to measure total voltage and total current at the ports of the network. 2. Short and open circuits are difficult to achieve over a broad band of frequencies. 3. Active devices, such as transistors and tunnel diodes, very often will not be short or open circuit stable"; "The logical variables to use at these frequencies are traveling waves rather than total voltages and currents." It defines reciprocity as S = Sᵀ and losslessness as a unitary S. The 2020 edition's equations in Figs. 11 and 13 contain subscript and sign typos absent from the 2000 Agilent edition; they are not reproduced.
- **F. Caspers, "RF engineering basic concepts: S-parameters," arXiv:1201.2346** [Established]: power waves a, b = (U ± I Z0)/(2√Z0); power formulas carry ½ with peak phasors; most passive components are reciprocal, magnetized ferrites being the exception.
- **P. Triverio, S. Grivet-Talocia, M. S. Nakhla, F. G. Canavero, R. Achar, "Stability, Causality, and Passivity in Electrical Interconnect Models," *IEEE Trans. Advanced Packaging* 30(4), Nov 2007, Theorem V.3** [Established; read from the page image]: a scattering matrix is passive if and only if it is causal, S(−jω) = S*(jω), and I − Sᴴ(jω)S(jω) is nonnegative-definite for all ω; a badly calibrated measured dataset violates it.
- **IBIS Open Forum, *Touchstone File Format Specification*, Version 2.0 (ratified 24 Apr 2009) and 2.1 (ratified 26 Jan 2024)** [Established]: "A Touchstone file (also known as an SnP file) is an ASCII text file used for documenting the n-port network parameter data"; "Files written using the original syntax … are referred to as Version 1.0 files, even though a Version 1.0 specification was never published"; default "MA format, GHz frequencies, 50-ohm reference, S-parameters".
- **Sources:** [Keysight AN 154](https://www.keysight.com/be/en/assets/7018-06743/application-notes/5952-1087.pdf); [Caspers, arXiv:1201.2346](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1201.2346); [Touchstone 2.1](https://ibis.org/touchstone_ver2.1/touchstone_ver2_1.pdf); [Triverio et al. (author copy)](https://iris.polito.it/retrieve/handle/11583/1662802/48869/jnl-2007-tadvp-Fundamentals-ieeexplore.pdf). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-141 · SerDes channel loss budgets

*Gathered by a research subagent (Course 07 facts file c0710, entries F-0833 to F-0842); documents opened unless stated.*

- **IEEE 802.3ck (100 Gb/s per lane) objectives, adopted by the Study Group Mar 2018** [Established]: "Define a single-lane 100 Gb/s PHY for operation over electrical backplanes supporting an insertion loss ≤ 28 dB at 26.56 GHz." Task-force contributions: Ghiasi (Sep 2019), "Max Bump-Bump Loss (dB) … ~36" for KR with "Package Losses (dB) … [4, 4]"; DiMinico (Nov 2019), "Channel IL =2*6.875(Host PCB and via's)+2*1.6(conn)+11.55(cable and wire termination) = 28.5 dB", cable assembly "Maximum insertion Loss 19.75 dB". The published 802.3ck-2022 normative text was not opened (paywalled).
- **IEEE P802.3dj (200 Gb/s per lane) objectives, 14 Mar 2024** [Established]: "over 1 lane over electrical backplanes supporting a die-to-die insertion loss <= 40 dB at 53.125 GHz", "Approved by IEEE 802.3 WG 18 May 2023"; Mellitz et al. (May 2023): "Why 40 dB? COMPROMISE!!!! … people building SERDES: 36-37dB IL target … people building systems: 42-45 dB IL target". Status: conditional approval to IEEE SA ballot, 3 Feb 2026; final approval not confirmed as of 19 Nov 2026.
- **PCI Express** [Industry estimate: work-group presentations; the specifications are members-only]: M. Mazumder (co-chair, PCI-SIG Electrical WG), SNIA SDC 2021: "Pad-to-pad channel loss < 32 dB at 16 GHz" for PCIe 6.0 (Rev 0.9) against −36 dB for PCIe 5.0, split "Root Complex (RC) … -8.0", "Add-in-Card (AIC) … -8.5", "System … -15.5"; SNIA SDC 2024: Nyquist 16 GHz (PCIe 5.0 and 6.0) and 32 GHz (PCIe 7.0); "PCB Loss at 16 GHz: ~ 1 dB/in"; "Cable Loss at 16 GHz: ~0.1875 dB/in". A "36 dB at 32 GHz" PCIe 7.0 figure appeared only in a trade article and a search excerpt and is not used.
- **Sources:** [802.3ck objectives](https://grouper.ieee.org/groups/802/3/ck/P802_3ck_Objectives_2018mar.pdf); [Ghiasi 2019](https://grouper.ieee.org/groups/802/3/ck/public/19_09/ghiasi_3ck_01a_0919.pdf); [DiMinico 2019](https://grouper.ieee.org/groups/802/3/ck/public/19_11/diminico_3ck_01_1119.pdf); [P802.3dj objectives](https://grouper.ieee.org/groups/802/3/dj/projdoc/objectives_P802d3dj_240314.pdf); [Mellitz et al. 2023](https://www.ieee802.org/3/dj/public/23_05/mellitz_3dj_01a_2305.pdf); [Mazumder, SNIA SDC 2021](https://snia.org/sites/default/files/2025-05/SNIA-SDC21-Mazumder-PCIe6p0-A-High-Performance-Interconnect-for-Storage-Networking-Challenges.pdf); [Mazumder, SNIA SDC 2024](https://www.snia.org/sites/default/files/2025-05/SNIA-SDC2024-Mazumder-PCIe-Cabling-Solutions_1.pdf). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-142 · Three-phase distribution voltages

*Gathered by a research subagent (Course 07 facts file, entries F-0733 to F-0735); documents opened. Extends R-006 and R-014.*

- **IEC 60038:2009, *IEC standard voltages*, Edition 7.0 (2009-06), preview (foreword and contents, VDE Verlag)** [Established for the edition and changes]: "This seventh edition supersedes the sixth edition (1993), its Amendment 1 (1994) and its Amendment 2 (1997)"; "the addition of the values of 230 V (50 Hz) and 230/400 V (60 Hz) to Table1; the update of Table 1 to take into account the end of the transition period for the values of 230/400 V and 400/690 V". Table 1 itself is not in the preview.
- **Legrand / Server Technology, *Power Efficiency Gains by Deploying 415 VAC Power Distribution in Data Centers*, white paper (©2022)** [Industry estimate: vendor claims]: "In North America, 277/480 VAC, 3-phase power is delivered to most large commercial buildings and IT facilities"; the baseline path through "an end-of-row Power Distribution Unit (PDU) transformer, which is stepped down from 480 VAC 3-phase to 208 VAC 3-phase"; the alternative "converting the UPS output from 277/480 VAC to 240/415 VAC through an autotransformer and eliminating the PDU transformer"; "eliminating the PDU transformer will result in a 2 percent efficiency gain" (attributed to UPS manufacturers); servers at 240 V gain "an added 0.2 percent to 0.3 percent" (example supply 92.8% at 208 V, 93.1% at 240 V). **[Contested: arithmetic]** Its Table 2 lists three-phase 208 V at 30 A as 10.8 kW for both full and 80% continuous rating (continuous should be 8.6 kW) and 208 V at 60 A as 17.3 kW in both columns (full should be 21.6 kW).
- **IEEE History Center (ETHW), "Milestones: Sakuma Frequency Converter Station, 1965" (dedication #305, 13 Oct 2026)** [Established]: "used a 300-megawatt frequency converter based on a mercury-arc rectifier to enable the first large-scale power exchange between Japan's eastern 50 Hz grid and western 60 Hz grid"; "In 1895, Tokyo Electric Light Company adopted a '50 Hz' generator manufactured by AEG of Germany. Two years later, Osaka Electric Light Company introduced a '60 Hz' generator from General Electric". Today's total converter capacity was not verified.
- **Course 07 audit update (19 Nov 2026), from web-search excerpts only:** IEC 60038:2009+AMD1:2021 CSV is the consolidated edition 7.1 (2021-12-15); per the IEC Webstore listing, the amendment adds a 30 kV value to Table 3 and replaces 1 050 kV with 1 100 kV in Table 5, so the low-voltage values above are unchanged [Established]. The Legrand/Server Technology paper's PDF is titled "…in Data Centers" but trade sites distribute it as "…in North American Data Centers" (Data Center Knowledge, 22 Dec 2021). The PDF host could not be reached during the audit, so the column placement of its 415 V rows was not re-checked.
- **Sources:** [IEC Webstore, IEC 60038 consolidated version](https://webstore.iec.ch/en/publication/72877); [Data Center Knowledge listing](https://www.datacenterknowledge.com/energy/power-efficiency-gains-deploying-415-vac-power-distribution-north-american-data-centers); [IEC 60038 preview](https://www.vde-verlag.de/iec-normen/preview-pdf/info_iec60038%7Bed7.0%7Db.pdf); [Legrand/Server Technology white paper](https://cdn10.servertech.com/assets/documents/documents/1023/original/DPC_STI_PowerEfficiencyGains_WhitePaper.pdf); [ETHW Sakuma Milestone](https://ethw.org/Milestones:Sakuma_Frequency_Converter_Station,_1965). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-143 · Block-scaled AI number formats

*Gathered by a research subagent (Course 08 facts file, entries F-0807 and F-0808) and cross-checked by web search on 19 Nov 2026. The OCP and NVIDIA hosts could not be reached from the build environment, so every item below is from search-result excerpts of the named pages; re-check against the primary documents when they can be opened.*

- **OCP Microscaling Formats (MX) Specification v1.0** (September 2023), announced by OCP on 17 Oct 2023 on behalf of AMD, Arm, Intel, Meta, Microsoft, NVIDIA and Qualcomm: "The initial specification introduces four concrete floating point and integer-based data formats (MXFP8, MXFP6, MXFP4, and MXINT8)". Element types: MXFP8 E5M2 or E4M3, MXFP6 E3M2 or E2M3, MXFP4 E2M1, MXINT8. Every concrete format uses blocks of 32 elements sharing one 8-bit power-of-two scale (E8M0), so MXFP4 costs 4 + 8/32 = 4.25 bits per element. The block size and scale format were read from secondary documentation (pychop and Intel Neural Compressor docs), consistent across sources [Established]. An "OCP_MicroXcaling_Spec_v1.1.0.pdf" was uploaded to the OCP site in Oct 2026 and was not read.
- **NVIDIA NVFP4** (NVIDIA Technical Blog, "Introducing NVFP4 for Efficient and Accurate Low-precision Inference", June 2025): 4-bit E2M1 values (about −6 to 6), one FP8 E4M3 scale per 16-value block and a second-level FP32 scale per tensor: "This second-level scaling factor is done at a per-tensor level with FP32, which adjusts the original tensor's distribution such that the micro-blocks can be effectively encoded using E4M3 scale factors." About 4.5 bits per value [Established]. NVIDIA claims an accuracy loss of 1% or less against FP8 on its chosen benchmarks (DeepSeek-R1-0528) [Industry estimate: vendor claim].
- **Sources:** [OCP MX announcement](https://www.opencompute.org/blog/amd-arm-intel-meta-microsoft-nvidia-and-qualcomm-standardize-next-generation-narrow-precision-data-formats-for-ai); [OCP MX v1.0 specification](https://opencompute.org/documents/ocp-microscaling-formats-mx-v1-0-spec-final-pdf); [pychop MX documentation](https://pychop.readthedocs.io/en/latest/ocp_mx.html); [Intel Neural Compressor, Microscaling Quantization](https://intel.github.io/neural-compressor/latest/docs/source/3x/PT_MXQuant.html); [NVIDIA NVFP4 blog](https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/introducing-nvfp4-for-efficient-and-accurate-low-precision-inference); [repost at edge-ai-vision.com](https://www.edge-ai-vision.com/2025/07/introducing-nvfp4-for-efficient-and-accurate-low-precision-inference/). Checked Nov 2026 (search excerpts).

## R-144 · DRAM error correction

*Course 08 facts file entries F-0813 and F-0814, cross-checked by web search on 19 Nov 2026; search-result excerpts only (JESD79-5 itself is paywalled).*

- **DDR5 on-die ECC:** Micron's *DDR5 New Features* white paper: "DDR5 SDRAM ECC is implemented as single error correction (SEC), pairing 128 data bits with 8 parity bits to form a 136-bit codeword." Academic papers describe it as a Hamming code with (n = 136, k = 128, d = 3), invisible to the system, able to miscorrect some double-bit errors into triple-bit errors, and separate from rank-level (side-band) ECC [Established].
- **JESD79-5C:** JEDEC announced the update on 17 Apr 2024: timings to 8800 Mbps, Per-Row Activation Counting (PRAC) against Rowhammer, and removal of Partial Array Self-Refresh for security reasons [Established]. Excerpts disagree on the previous speed ceiling, so none is quoted.
- **DDR5 module organization (added 19 Nov 2026 after the Course 08 audit):** the Wikipedia article *DDR5 SDRAM* (read directly): "Both subchannels on a DDR5 DIMM each have their own CA bus, controlling 32 bits for non-ECC memory and either 36 or 40 data lines for ECC memory, resulting in a total number of either 64, 72 or 80 data lines." DDR4 and earlier modules had one channel of 64 (non-ECC) or 72 (ECC) data lines [Established; secondary summary of JESD79-5, which is paywalled]. The Synopsys ECC bulletin (read directly) confirms that standard-DDR systems use side-band ECC on separate DRAMs and that on-die ECC is used "in conjunction with side-band ECC". The 25% overhead of 8 check bits per 32 data bits and the 7-bit SEC-DED requirement for 32 data bits are derived in 08-01.
- **Sources:** [Wikipedia, DDR5 SDRAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DDR5_SDRAM); [Micron DDR5 New Features white paper](https://www.micron.cn/content/dam/micron/global/public/products/white-paper/ddr5-new-features-white-paper.pdf); [Synopsys, Key Features of DDR5](https://www.synopsys.com/designware-ip/technical-bulletin/ddr5-features.html); [Synopsys, Error Correction Code (ECC) in DDR Memories](https://www.synopsys.com/designware-ip/technical-bulletin/error-correction-code-ddr.html); [Patel et al., DSN 2019](https://people.cs.rutgers.edu/~mp2099/files/patel_dsn19_understanding_paper.pdf); [JEDEC JESD79-5C release (Business Wire)](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20240417886230/en/JEDEC-Updates-JESD79-5C-DDR5-SDRAM-Standard-Elevating-Performance-and-Security-for-Next-Gen-Technologies). Checked Nov 2026 (search excerpts).

## R-145 · Ethernet CRC-32

*Course 08 facts file entries F-0811 and F-0812, cross-checked by web search on 19 Nov 2026; search-result excerpts only.*

- **Frame check sequence:** IEEE 802.3 clause 3.2.9 uses G(x) = x³² + x²⁶ + x²³ + x²² + x¹⁶ + x¹² + x¹¹ + x¹⁰ + x⁸ + x⁷ + x⁵ + x⁴ + x² + x + 1 (04C11DB7₁₆ for the terms below x³²). The first 32 bits are complemented, M(x)·x³² is divided by G(x), and the remainder is complemented; the FCS covers addresses, length/type, data and pad. Quoted via Intel's Triple-Speed Ethernet IP user guide; the IEEE text itself was not read [Established].
- **Hamming distance:** P. Koopman, "32-Bit Cyclic Redundancy Codes for Internet Applications," IEEE DSN 2002, the first exhaustive search of 32-bit CRC polynomials: the IEEE 802.3 CRC-32 detects up to three independent bit errors in a 1500-byte payload (HD = 4), and other polynomials reach higher HD at those lengths [Established]. Taken from abstracts and excerpts; the paper's tables were not opened.
- **Sources:** [Intel Triple-Speed Ethernet IP user guide, CRC checking](https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/docs/programmable/683402/24-3/crc-checking.html); [Koopman 2002, CMU repository](https://kilthub.cmu.edu/articles/journal_contribution/32-Bit_Cyclic_Redundancy_Codes_for_Internet_Applications/6620594); [Koopman 2002, copy](https://typeset.io/pdf/32-bit-cyclic-redundancy-codes-for-internet-applications-1vyanily1r.pdf). Checked Nov 2026 (search excerpts).

## R-148 · Standard-cell library structure (SKY130)

*Read directly from the repositories with `git` on 19 Nov 2026 (the netlists were also read by the Course 08 research subagent, facts F-0815 to F-0818).*

- **SkyWater SKY130 high-density library (sky130_fd_sc_hd)**, repository google/skywater-pdk-libs-sky130_fd_sc_hd at commit ac7fb61f06e6470b94e8afdf7c25268f62fbd7b1 (10 Nov 2020) [Established]:
  - `inv_1`: "X0 VGND A Y VNB sky130_fd_pr__nfet_01v8 w=650000u l=150000u" and "X1 VPWR A Y VPB sky130_fd_pr__pfet_01v8_hvt w=1e+06u l=150000u": one nMOS and one high-threshold pMOS, 0.65 µm and 1.0 µm wide at 0.15 µm length (dimensions in µm under the PDK's scale convention).
  - `nand2_1`: two parallel pMOS (w = 1.0 µm) from VPWR to Y, and two series nMOS (w = 0.65 µm) through the internal node `a_113_47#` to VGND: 4 transistors.
  - `nor2_1`: two series pMOS (w = 1.0 µm) through `a_109_297#`, and two parallel nMOS (w = 0.65 µm): 4 transistors. The series pMOS are the same width as the NAND2's parallel pMOS.
  - `nand2_2` has 8 device lines (two copies of the NAND2); the library has nand2 in drives _1, _2, _4, _8 and inv in _1, _2, _4, _6, _8, _12, _16; the D flip-flop `dfxtp_1` has 24 device lines.
- **google/skywater-pdk README** (commit 7198cf6, 29 May 2023, the repository's latest): "Google and SkyWater are currently treating the current content as an **experimental preview** / **alpha release**." Maintained PDK packaging continues in the open_pdks project (RTimothyEdwards/open_pdks, tag 1.0.619, Nov 2026) [Established].
- **Sources:** [sky130_fd_sc_hd repository](https://github.com/google/skywater-pdk-libs-sky130_fd_sc_hd); [google/skywater-pdk](https://github.com/google/skywater-pdk); [open_pdks](https://github.com/RTimothyEdwards/open_pdks). Checked Nov 2026.

Brayton and A. Mishchenko, "ABC: An Academic Industrial-Strength Verification Tool," CAV 2010, LNCS 6174, pp. 24–40. Built on and-inverter graphs [Established].

It supports an (informally defined) synthesizable subset of SystemVerilog in version IEEE 1800-2017 or IEEE 1800-2023."; "Yosys is free software licensed under the ISC license"; the source tree includes `abc`, and cell-library mapping uses `abc -liberty mycells.lib` [Established].

Sangiovanni-Vincentelli, *Logic Minimization Algorithms for VLSI Synthesis*, Kluwer Academic, 1984; the book's front matter: "work on logic synthesis started in 1979 at the IBM Watson Research Center and at University of California, Berkeley". Successor Espresso-MV: R. Rudell, UCB/ERL M86/65 (1986) [Established; search excerpts].

## R-155 · PRBS and LFSR references

*From search excerpts (vendor and IEEE pages unreachable from the build environment), Course 08 facts file entries F-0841 to F-0843; Swan's theorem from a search excerpt of a later paper's abstract. That each PRBS polynomial below is primitive, and that no degree-8 trinomial is irreducible, is proved independently by `verify_08_07.py`.*

- **Standard PRBS polynomials:** PRBS7 $x^7 + x^6 + 1$, PRBS15 $x^{15} + x^{14} + 1$, PRBS23 $x^{23} + x^{18} + 1$, PRBS31 $x^{31} + x^{28} + 1$, as tabulated in Intel's E-Tile Transceiver PHY User Guide ("Data Pattern Generation") and AMD transceiver documentation, which attribute them to ITU-T O.150 / O.151; several O.150 sequences are defined inverted [Established; search excerpts, tables not read verbatim].
- **IEEE 802.3 PRBS31:** defined as a test pattern for 10GBASE-R in clause 49.2.8; 802.3ae-era reflector discussion: "PRBS31 differs from O.150 in that for every 1 in O.150, D4.1 has a 0" (the same polynomial, bit-inverted). IEEE P802.3dj (2025) material carries PRBS31 and its PAM4 form PRBS31Q into the 200 Gb/s-per-lane generation [Established; search excerpts, the clause text itself not seen].
- **Xilinx XAPP052:** P. Alfke, "Efficient Shift Registers, LFSR Counters, and Long Pseudo-Random Sequence Generators", version 1.1, 7 July 1996, written for the XC4000; tabulates maximal-length taps up to 168 bits, "based on unpublished research done by Wayne Stahnke while he was at Fairchild Semiconductor in 1970" [Established; search excerpt of university copies, individual tap entries not checked against the PDF].
- **Swan's theorem:** R. G. Swan, "Factorization of polynomials over finite fields", *Pacific Journal of Mathematics* 12(3), 1962, pp. 1099–1106. A later abstract (Bluher) summarizes: Swan proved that trinomials $x^{8k} + x^m + 1$ with $8k > m$ have an even number of irreducible factors, so they cannot be irreducible [Established; summary from the later abstract, Swan's own statement not read].
- **Sources:** [Intel E-Tile PHY User Guide, Data Pattern Generation](https://intel.com/content/www/us/en/docs/programmable/683723/current/data-pattern-generation.html); [AMD transceiver document](https://docs.amd.com/api/khub/documents/gYlAK_4raq3aAZ_rFGSPmQ/content); [IEEE 802.3dj presentation (Dudek, Mar 2025)](https://ieee802.org/3/dj/public/25_03/dudek_3dj_01_2503.pdf); [802.3ae reflector message](https://grouper.ieee.org/groups/802/3/10G_study/email/msg05450.html); [XAPP052, University of Idaho copy](https://www.webpages.uidaho.edu/~jfrenzel/440/Handouts/lfsr_XAPP052.pdf); [Swan 1962, Pacific J. Math.](https://msp.org/pjm/1962/12-3/p27.xhtml); [Bluher, arXiv math/0406538](https://www.arxiv.org/abs/math/0406538). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-156 · FSM synthesis and protocol state machines

*Yosys facts read from its repository with `git` (Course 08 facts file F-0845, F-0846); SKY130 `dfrtp_1` area read from the library's Liberty data with `git` on 19 Nov 2026; Vivado and PCIe facts from search excerpts (vendor and PCI-SIG pages unreachable from the build environment), F-0844 and two searches on 19 Nov 2026.*

- **Yosys FSM flow** (YosysHQ/yosys, commit d1338f8, `docs/source/using_yosys/synthesis/fsm.rst` and `passes/fsm/`): "The `fsm` command identifies, extracts, optimizes (re-encodes), and re-synthesizes finite state machines." `fsm_detect` marks a register as an FSM state register when it is not a module output, is driven by a single flip-flop cell, its next value comes from a multiplexer tree whose leaves are constants or the old state, and it is only compared with constants; `fsm_encoding = "none"` or `"auto"` suppresses or forces detection [Established].
- **Yosys automatic re-encoding** (`passes/fsm/fsm_recode.cc`): `encoding = GetSize(fsm_data.state_table) < 32 ? "one-hot" : "binary";` and "At the moment only one-hot encoding and binary encoding is supported." [Established].
- **AMD Vivado `FSM_ENCODING`** (UG901, UG912): values one_hot, sequential, johnson, gray, user_encoding, none and auto (the default, the tool chooses); "NONE disables state machine encoding within the Vivado synthesis tool for the specified state machine registers. In this case the state machine is synthesized as logic." [Established; search excerpts of the 2022–2023 guides].
- **AMD Vivado `FSM_SAFE_STATE`** (UG901 2025.2, UG912 2025.2): adds logic that detects an illegal state and moves the machine to a known state on the next cycle. `auto_safe_state` "Uses Hamming-3 encoding for auto-correction for one bit flip"; `reset_state`, `power_on_state` and `default_state` force the named state "using Hamming-2 encoding detection for one bit flip"; set on the state registers in RTL or XDC (`set_property fsm_safe_state reset_state [get_cells state_reg*]`). The value list differs between Vivado versions (2022.1 lists fewer values) [Established; search excerpts].
- **SkyWater SKY130 `sky130_fd_sc_hd__dfrtp_1`** (flip-flop with active-low asynchronous reset; google/skywater-pdk-libs-sky130_fd_sc_hd, commit ac7fb61, `cells/dfrtp/sky130_fd_sc_hd__dfrtp_1__tt_025C_1v80.lib.json`): area 25.024 µm² (the `nand2_1` is 3.7536 µm², R-154) [Established].
- **PCIe LTSSM:** a PCI Express textbook lists eleven top-level LTSSM states, Detect, Polling, Configuration, Recovery, L0, L0s, L1, L2, Hot Reset, Loopback and Disabled, with Detect the first state after a fundamental or hot reset; vendor documentation (Intel, TI, Efinix) lists sub-states such as Detect.Quiet and Polling.Active and exposes the current LTSSM state in monitor registers, with vendor-specific encodings [Established; search excerpts of secondary sources; the PCI-SIG base specification is members-only and was not read].
- **Sources:** [YosysHQ/yosys](https://github.com/YosysHQ/yosys); [AMD UG901 FSM_ENCODING (2023.1)](https://docs.amd.com/r/2023.1-English/ug901-vivado-synthesis/FSM_ENCODING); [AMD UG912 FSM_ENCODING](https://docs.amd.com/r/en-US/ug912-vivado-properties/FSM_ENCODING); [AMD UG901 FSM_SAFE_STATE (2025.2)](https://docs.amd.com/r/en-US/ug901-vivado-synthesis/FSM_SAFE_STATE); [AMD UG912 FSM_SAFE_STATE (2025.2)](https://docs.amd.com/r/2025.2-English/ug912-vivado-properties/FSM_SAFE_STATE); [sky130_fd_sc_hd repository](https://github.com/google/skywater-pdk-libs-sky130_fd_sc_hd); [PCI Express System Architecture, LTSSM section (O'Reilly)](https://oreilly.com/library/view/pci-express-system/0321156307/0321156307_ch14lev1sec6.html); [Intel PCIe IP user guide, LTSSM monitor registers](https://intel.com/content/www/us/en/docs/programmable/683667/23-4/ltssm-monitor-registers.html); [Efinix LTSSM state encoding](https://www.efinixinc.com/docs-html/xcvr-pcie-controller-ug/topics/pcie/app-ltssm_state_encoding.html). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-157 · Arithmetic energy and DSP blocks

*Horowitz figure read directly from the paper (PDF downloaded with `curl` through the build environment's proxy on 19 Nov 2026 and its page rendered to an image, because the table is a figure); the other facts from search excerpts (Course 08 facts file F-0847 to F-0849, and a search on 19 Nov 2026).*

- **Energy per operation, 45 nm, 0.9 V** (M. Horowitz, "1.1 Computing's energy problem (and what we can do about it)", ISSCC 2014, DOI 10.1109/ISSCC.2014.6757323, Figure 1.1.9 "Rough energy costs for various operations in 45nm 0.9V"): integer add 8 bit 0.03 pJ, 32 bit 0.1 pJ; integer multiply 8 bit 0.2 pJ, 32 bit 3.1 pJ; floating-point add 16 bit 0.4 pJ, 32 bit 0.9 pJ; floating-point multiply 16 bit 1.1 pJ, 32 bit 3.7 pJ; 64-bit cache access 8 KB 10 pJ, 32 KB 20 pJ, 1 MB 100 pJ; DRAM 1.3–2.6 nJ; instruction energy breakdown about 70 pJ in total (I-cache access 25 pJ, register file 6 pJ, control, with the add a small sliver) [Established; read from the figure].
- **Kogge–Stone:** P. M. Kogge and H. S. Stone, "A Parallel Algorithm for the Efficient Solution of a General Class of Recurrence Equations," *IEEE Transactions on Computers* C-22(8), 786–793, Aug. 1973; recursive doubling "in time proportional to log2 N on a computer with N-fold parallelism" [Established; search excerpt].
- **Brent–Kung:** R. P. Brent and H. T. Kung, "A Regular Layout for Parallel Adders," *IEEE Transactions on Computers* C-31(3), 260–264, Mar. 1982 (earlier as CMU-CS-79-131, 1979): "addition of n-bit binary numbers can be done on a chip with a regular layout in time proportional to log n" [Established; search excerpt].
- **Prefix-network families:** D. Harris, "A taxonomy of parallel prefix networks" (IEEE Xplore document 1292373) describes a three-dimensional taxonomy of logic levels, fan-out and wiring tracks that places Kogge–Stone, Sklansky, Brent–Kung and the Knowles family in one space; S. Knowles, "A family of adders" (IEEE Symposium on Computer Arithmetic) [Established for the titles and authors; the years and venues were inconsistent across search excerpts (Knowles cited as 1999 and 2001), so the chapter gives no dates].
- **AMD DSP slices:** the UltraScale and UltraScale+ DSP48E2 has a 27 × 18 signed multiplier (25 × 18 in the 7-series DSP48E1), a 27-bit pre-adder and a 48-bit accumulator; UG974 calls it a "48-bit Multi-Functional Arithmetic Block"; the Versal DSP58 multiplier is 27 × 24 [Established; search excerpts of AMD documentation; UG579 itself not read].
- **Sources:** [Horowitz ISSCC 2014 (PDF copy)](https://www.gwern.net/doc/cs/hardware/2014-horowitz-2.pdf); [Kogge–Stone BibTeX record](https://researchr.org/publication/KoggeS73/bibtex); [Brent publication page](https://maths-people.anu.edu.au/~brent/pub/pub060.html); [Harris taxonomy, IEEE Xplore](https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/abstract/document/1292373); [AMD DSP technology page](https://www.amd.com/en/products/adaptive-socs-and-fpgas/technologies/digital-signal-processing.html); [AMD UG974 DSP48E2](https://docs.amd.com/r/en-US/ug974-vivado-ultrascale-libraries/DSP48E2). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-158 · Metastability and synchronizers

*Beer and Ginosar papers read directly (PDFs downloaded with `curl` on 19 Nov 2026; τ values read from the 2012 paper's figures); Altera, Intel, AMD and Microchip facts from search excerpts (Course 08 facts file F-0851 to F-0855); Ginosar 2011 and Cummings 2002 bibliographic details from a search on 19 Nov 2026.*

- **MTBF law (Beer and Ginosar):** S. Beer and R. Ginosar, "A Model for Supply Voltage and Temperature Variation Effects on Synchronizer Performance," *IEEE Transactions on VLSI Systems* 23(11), 2461–2472, Nov. 2015, DOI 10.1109/TVLSI.2014.2365255. Equation (1): MTBF $= e^{S/\tau}/(T_W \times F_C \times F_D)$, with $F_C$ and $F_D$ the clock and data transition frequencies, $S$ the time allowed for resolution, $\tau$ the resolution time constant and $T_W$ "a vulnerable time window which is determined experimentally"; the abstract notes "degradation of MTBF with technology scaling for library flip-flop circuits in 65 nm and below" [Established; read from the paper].
- **Measured τ (Beer and Ginosar, 2012):** S. Beer and R. Ginosar, "A new 65nm LP metastability measurement test circuit," 2012 IEEE 27th Convention of Electrical and Electronics Engineers in Israel. Figures 5 and 6 plot the interpolated measured τ of a library flip-flop over 0.95–1.25 V and −20 to 100 °C: from below about 100 ps at the higher voltages and temperatures to roughly 700 ps at 0.95 V and −20 °C [Established; values read approximately from the paper's 3-D and color-map figures]. A search excerpt attributing to Beer and Ginosar the sentence "The τ measured on the 65nm circuit was about 100ps, in contrast with expectations of less than 30ps" was not found in either paper read, so the chapter does not use it.
- **Altera WP-01082,** "Understanding Metastability in FPGAs": MTBF $= e^{t_{MET}/C_2}/(C_1 f_{CLK} f_{DATA})$, where "The tMET parameter is the available metastability settling time, or the timing slack available beyond the registers tCO"; for a register chain, tMET is the sum of the per-register slacks; Quartus identifies synchronizer chains and reports MTBF [Established; search excerpts; the equation's typography reconstructed from the excerpt's description].
- **Intel Quartus Prime Pro design recommendations (25.1):** "designers commonly use two registers to synchronize a new signal, but a standard of three registers provides better metastability protection" (excerpt; may be paraphrased) [Established].
- **AMD XPM CDC macros (UG974, UG949):** DEST_SYNC_FF sets the number of destination synchronizer stages, from 2 to 10, default 4; "The value of this register influences MTBF, design size, and latency at the crossing point" [Established; search excerpts].
- **Microchip AN6287** (formerly AC474), "RTG4 and PolarFire Family Metastability Characterization Report," characterizes RTG4, PolarFire and RT PolarFire flip-flops with an MTBF model [Established; search excerpt; the measured values were not extracted].
- **Further-study references:** R. Ginosar, "Metastability and Synchronizers: A Tutorial," *IEEE Design & Test of Computers* 28(5), 23–35, 2011, DOI 10.1109/MDT.2011.113; C. E. Cummings, "Simulation and Synthesis Techniques for Asynchronous FIFO Design," SNUG San Jose 2002 [Established; search excerpts].
- **Sources:** [Beer and Ginosar 2015 (author copy)](https://webee.technion.ac.il/~ran/papers/VT-var-effects-on-Sync-BeerGinosar2015.pdf); [Beer and Ginosar 2012 (author copy)](https://webee.technion.ac.il/~ran/papers/BeerNew65nmLPMetastabilityMeasurmentIEEE-Israel2012.pdf); [Altera WP-01082](https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/content-details/650346/understanding-metastability-in-fpgas.html); [Intel Quartus metastability analysis (25.1)](https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/docs/programmable/683082/25-1/metastability-analysis-in-the-software.html); [AMD UG974 XPM_CDC_SINGLE](https://docs.amd.com/r/2025.2-English/ug974-vivado-ultrascale-libraries/XPM_CDC_SINGLE); [AMD UG949 DEST_SYNC_FF](https://docs.amd.com/r/2023.1-English/ug949-vivado-design-methodology/Selecting-the-Correct-Value-for-the-DEST_SYNC_FF-Parameter); [Microchip AN6287](https://www.microchip.com/en-us/application-notes/an6287); [Ginosar 2011 (author copy)](https://www.ee.technion.ac.il/~ran/papers/Metastability-and-Synchronizers.IEEEDToct2011.pdf). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-159 · FPGA devices and the FPGA industry

*The Lattice data sheet read directly (a university-hosted copy of version 2.0, downloaded with `curl` on 19 Nov 2026); the other facts from search excerpts (Course 08 facts file F-0856 to F-0860).*

- **Lattice iCE40 UltraPlus** (FPGA-DS-02008 version 2.0; the current version is 2.4, March 2025, per an excerpt): "Two devices with 2800 to 5280 LUTs", "fabricated in a 40 nm Low Power CMOS process"; for the UP5K: 5280 logic cells (LUT + flip-flop) in programmable logic blocks of 8 cells with a 4-input LUT and carry logic; 30 EBR blocks totaling 120 kbit (RAM4K blocks of 4 kbit); 4 SPRAM blocks totaling 1024 kbit, each "16K x 16"; 8 DSP blocks (MULT16 with 32-bit accumulator) [Established; read from the data sheet]. The data sheet's EBR table lists the block configurations 256 × 16, 512 × 8, 1024 × 4 and 2048 × 2 (each 4 kbit), so the narrowest port is 2 bits wide (added Nov 2026 after the Course 08 audit) [Established].
- **AMD Virtex UltraScale+ VU19P:** "System Logic Cells (K) 8,938", 4,086K CLB LUTs, 8,172K CLB flip-flops, "DSP Slices 3,840", "Memory (Mb) 224" (90 Mb UltraRAM), 80 GTY transceivers, 2,072 I/O; announced by Xilinx in August 2019 as the "world's largest FPGA" [Established; search excerpts of AMD's product table and 2019 coverage]. The 774× LUT ratio to the UP5K is arithmetic ($4\,086\,000/5280$).
- **Altera and Silver Lake:** on 14 April 2025 Intel agreed to sell 51% of Altera to Silver Lake at a USD 8.75 billion valuation, retaining 49%; the deal closed 12 September 2025, announced 15 September 2025 ("world's largest pure-play FPGA solutions provider"); Intel had acquired Altera for USD 16.7 billion in 2015; Altera FY2024 revenue USD 1.54 billion [Established]; reported cash proceeds to Intel differ (about 3.3, 3.4 or 4.46 billion USD) [Contested].
- **Vendor revenues:** AMD's Embedded segment FY2025 revenue about USD 3.5 billion, down 3% (AMD total USD 34.6 billion; released 3 February 2026; the segment also contains non-FPGA embedded processors); Lattice FY2025 (ended 3 January 2026) revenue USD 523.3 million, up about 2.7% from USD 509.4 million [Established; company releases via search excerpts].
- **Market-size estimates for 2025:** Mordor Intelligence USD 10.08 billion; MarketsandMarkets USD 11.73 billion (USD 19.34 billion by 2030); Knowledge Sourcing USD 11.41 billion; GMI Insights USD 13.8 billion [Industry estimate; Contested]. A bottom-up sum of AMD Embedded, Lattice and Altera (mixing fiscal years, before Microchip and others) is about USD 5.5 billion, roughly half the top-down figures.
- **Sources:** [iCE40 UltraPlus data sheet v2.0 (university copy)](https://pages.hmc.edu/brake/class/e155/fa23/assets/doc/FPGA-DS-02008-2-0-iCE40-UltraPlus-Family-Data-Sheet.pdf); [Lattice iCE40 UltraPlus page](https://www.latticesemi.com/products/fpgaandcpld/ice40ultraplus); [AMD VU19P page](https://www.amd.com/en/products/adaptive-socs-and-fpgas/fpga/virtex-ultrascale-plus-vu19p.html); [AnandTech, 2019](https://anandtech.com/print/14798/xilinx-announces-world-largest-fpga-virtex-ultrascale-vu19p-with-9m-cells); [Altera press release, 14 Apr 2025](https://docs.altera.com/v/u/fLe5nSEw4hHOBK8c5lk4JA); [Business Wire, 15 Sep 2025](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250915168341/en/Altera-Closes-Silver-Lake-Investment-to-Become-Worlds-Largest-Pure-play-FPGA-Solutions-Provider); [AMD Q4 and FY2025 results](https://ir.amd.com/news-events/press-releases/detail/1276/amd-reports-fourth-quarter-and-full-year-2025-financial); [Lattice FY2025 results (SEC exhibit)](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/855658/000143774926003634/ex_882667.htm); [MarketsandMarkets](https://www.marketsandmarkets.com/PressReleases/fpga.asp); [GMI Insights](https://www.gminsights.com/industry-analysis/field-programmable-gate-array-fpga-market-size). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-160 · Open-source FPGA and ASIC flows

*Repository facts read with `git` (Course 08 facts file F-0861 to F-0867); Efabless and ChipFoundry from search excerpts (F-0868); tool versions also confirmed by installing them (`pip show`: yowasp-yosys 0.71.0.0.post1285, yowasp-nextpnr-ice40 0.11.1.0.post826) and running them for the 08-12 lab on 19 Nov 2026; configuration times read from the iCE40 UltraPlus data sheet v2.0.*

- **Yosys** (YosysHQ/yosys): annotated tags v0.70 (2026-10-13) and v0.71 (2026-11-12), a roughly monthly cadence; binary distribution via OSS CAD Suite [Established].
- **nextpnr** (YosysHQ/nextpnr, README at commit 0f387c6): "nextpnr aims to be a vendor neutral, timing driven, FOSS FPGA place and route tool"; supports iCE40 (Project IceStorm), ECP5 (Project Trellis), Nexus (Project Oxide), Gowin (Project Apicula), NanoXplore NG-Ultra and Cologne Chip GateMate; Cyclone V, MachXO2 and Xilinx 7-series (Project X-Ray) experimental; latest tag nextpnr-0.11.1 (commit dated 2026-08-10) [Established].
- **Project IceStorm** (YosysHQ/icestorm): "Project IceStorm aims at documenting the bitstream format of Lattice iCE40 FPGAs and providing simple tools for analyzing and creating bitstream files"; tag v1.1 created 2025-09-22 [Established].
- **Project Apicula** (YosysHQ/apicula): open Gowin bitstream tools, `pip install apycula`, used with Yosys and nextpnr-himbaechel; latest tag 0.35 (commit dated 2026-10-21) [Established].
- **Project X-Ray and F4PGA:** the prjxray README states that only Vivado 2017.2 works for its fuzzers ("**Is 2017.2 really required?** Yes, only `2017.2` works."); last commit 2025-06-05; the F4PGA top-level repository's last commit 2024-10-15 [Established]. That open flows are mature for small Lattice and Gowin parts and immature for large AMD and Altera parts is an interpretation of these facts [Industry estimate].
- **OpenROAD** (The-OpenROAD-Project/OpenROAD README at commit e1b501f): "The OpenROAD flow delivers an Autonomous, No-Human-In-Loop (NHIL) flow, 24 hour turnaround from RTL-GDSII"; quarterly release tags 26Q3 (2026-07-01) and 26Q4 (2026-10-01) [Established for the releases; the 24-hour figure is the project's own claim].
- **LibreLane** (librelane/librelane): "LibreLane is based on OpenLane 2 by Efabless Corporation (assets owned by UmbraLogic Technologies LLC)"; Apache-2.0, FOSSi Foundation trademark; stable tag 3.0.17 (2026-10-26) [Established].
- **Efabless and ChipFoundry:** Efabless, which ran the chipIgnite SKY130 multi-project-wafer shuttles used by Tiny Tapeout, shut down "Due to funding challenges" in the first quarter of 2025 (exact date unconfirmed); UmbraLogic Technologies LLC, operating as ChipFoundry, announced on 3 September 2025 that it had acquired Efabless's IP and technology assets [Established; search excerpts of several outlets].
- **iCE40 UltraPlus configuration time** (data sheet FPGA-DS-02008 v2.0, Table 4.23, "SPI Master or NVCM Configuration Time"): tCONFIG, POR/CRESET_B to device I/O active, maximum 140 ms at the default low frequency and 50 ms at medium frequency (the high-frequency value was blank in the extracted text); the family has internal non-volatile configuration memory (NVCM) and a 48 MHz high-frequency oscillator; the sysDSP block has "Optional independent pipeline control on Input Register, Output Register, and Intermediate Reg" [Established; read from the data sheet].
- **Sources:** [YosysHQ/yosys](https://github.com/YosysHQ/yosys); [YosysHQ/nextpnr](https://github.com/YosysHQ/nextpnr); [YosysHQ/icestorm](https://github.com/YosysHQ/icestorm); [YosysHQ/apicula](https://github.com/YosysHQ/apicula); [f4pga/prjxray](https://github.com/f4pga/prjxray); [chipsalliance/f4pga](https://github.com/chipsalliance/f4pga); [The-OpenROAD-Project/OpenROAD](https://github.com/The-OpenROAD-Project/OpenROAD); [librelane/librelane](https://github.com/librelane/librelane); [Tiny Tapeout news](https://tinytapeout.com/news/efabless-shutsdown/); [ChipFoundry release, GlobeNewswire](https://www.globenewswire.com/news-release/2025/09/03/3143962/0/en/chipfoundry-acquires-efabless-assets-to-propel-custom-silicon-innovation.html); [iCE40 UltraPlus data sheet v2.0 (university copy)](https://pages.hmc.edu/brake/class/e155/fa23/assets/doc/FPGA-DS-02008-2-0-iCE40-UltraPlus-Family-Data-Sheet.pdf). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-161 · Crystal, band-structure and alloy parameters

*Read from downloaded copies of the handbook and the III–V compilation on 19 Nov 2026 (Course 09 facts file F-0901 to F-0914, F-0948); page images checked where the text extraction was garbled. Derived values are recomputed by `courses/09-semiconductor-devices/verify/verify_09_01.py`.*

- **Silicon (Levinshtein & Rumyantsev, §1.1–1.2):** "Effective electron masses … longitudinal 0.98 … transverse 0.19"; "Effective hole masses … heavy 0.49 … light 0.16"; "Electron affinity (eV) 4.05"; "Lattice constant (A) 5.431"; "Energy gap (eV) 1.12"; "Energy spin-orbital splitting (eV) 0.044"; "Effective conduction band density of states (cm⁻³) 3.2 x 10¹⁹", valence 1.8 × 10¹⁹; density-of-states masses "1.18 m0" (conduction, six valleys of 0.36 m0 each) and "mv = 0.81 m0 (300 K)"; conductivity mass 0.26 m0; split-off band mass 0.24 m0; Varshni fit "Eg = 1.17 − 4.73 × 10⁻⁴ T²/(T + 636) (eV)" (eq. 1.2.1) [Established].
- **Germanium (§2.1):** gap 0.661 eV; lattice constant 5.658 Å; density 5.3234 g/cm³; masses longitudinal 1.6, transverse 0.08, heavy hole 0.33, light hole 0.043 m0; four equivalent conduction valleys; electron affinity 4.0 eV [Established].
- **Gallium arsenide (§4.1–4.2):** gap 1.424 eV; electron mass 0.063 m0; heavy and light hole masses 0.51 and 0.082 m0; electron affinity 4.07 eV; Γ–L and Γ–X valley separations 0.29 and 0.48 eV; N_c 4.7 × 10¹⁷ cm⁻³ [Established].
- **Handbook mobilities (§1.1, §4.1):** lightly doped Si electrons ≤ 1400 and holes ≤ 450 cm²/V·s; GaAs electrons ≤ 8500 and holes ≤ 400 cm²/V·s [Established].
- **Vurgaftman, Meyer & Ram-Mohan (2001):** GaAs (Table I) a = 5.65325 Å, Γ gap 1.519 eV at 0 K, α = 0.5405 meV/K, β = 204 K, X gap 1.981 eV; electron mass "a low-temperature result of 0.067m0 at the band edge… At room temperature, the currently accepted value is 0.0635m0"; AlAs (Table II) a = 5.6611 Å, Γ gap 3.099 eV (α 0.885 meV/K, β 530 K), X gap 2.24 eV; InAs (Table III) a = 6.0583 Å, Γ gap 0.417 eV (α 0.276 meV/K, β 93 K); InP (Table VI) a = 5.8697 Å [Established].
- **AlGaAs (§V.A.1, Table XII):** "Bowing parameters from 0.14 to 0.66 eV have been proposed for the Γ-valley energy gap"; recommended C = (−0.127 + 1.310x) eV after Aspnes et al.; "Γ–X crossover composition of x = 0.38 at low temperatures (and 0.39 at 300 K)" [Established for a crossover near 0.4; Contested for the bowing]. Note: evaluating the recommended parameters directly puts the 0 K crossover nearer x ≈ 0.42, so the chapter quotes only "about 0.4" and makes no numerical AlGaAs gap claim. The often-quoted linear form E_g = 1.424 + 1.247x (x < 0.45), attributed to Casey and Panish, could not be read in a primary source (open issue).
- **GaInAs (§V.A.2):** "we choose a composite average of Eg(T=0) = 816 meV, which in turn implies a bowing parameter C = 0.477 eV" for Ga0.47In0.53As lattice-matched to InP; bowing assumed independent of temperature [Established]. Derived here: 0.7373 eV and 1682 nm at 300 K.
- **Wide-gap gaps:** GaN (wurtzite) 3.507 eV at 0 K with α = 0.909 meV/K, β = 830 K (Vurgaftman Table X), giving 3.435 eV at 300 K (derived); the Ioffe database lists 3.39 eV (search excerpt only); 4H-SiC 3.2, 3.23 or 3.26 eV (R-086) [Contested beyond the first decimal].
- **Hu, Table 1–4:** N_c 2.8 × 10¹⁹ and N_v 1.04 × 10¹⁹ cm⁻³ for Si; 1.04 × 10¹⁹ and 6.0 × 10¹⁸ for Ge; 4.7 × 10¹⁷ and 7.0 × 10¹⁸ for GaAs [Established].
- **Sources:** M. E. Levinshtein, S. L. Rumyantsev and M. S. Shur (eds.), *Handbook Series on Semiconductor Parameters, Vol. 1* (World Scientific, 1996), read from a library copy at [bdt.semi.ac.cn](https://bdt.semi.ac.cn/library/upload/files/2020/7/14133813301.pdf) (cite the book); [Vurgaftman et al., J. Appl. Phys. 89, 5815 (2001), course copy](https://ursemiconductors.web.illinois.edu/ECE536/Vurgaftman(2001).pdf), DOI 10.1063/1.1368156; [C. Hu, ch. 1–3](https://www.chu.berkeley.edu/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/Chenming-Hu_ch1-3.pdf). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-162 · Wafer orientation, strain and wafer size

*Primary documents read on 19 Nov 2026 (Course 09 facts file F-0915 to F-0917, F-0920), plus Crossref bibliographic records for the Intel strained-silicon papers (titles, venues and pages only; the papers were not read).*

- **Orientation (Hu §1.1):** "silicon wafers are usually cut along the (100) plane to obtain uniformity and good device performance. A flat or a notch is cut along the (011) plane"; "Different surface orientations have different properties such as the rate of oxidation and the electronic quality of the oxide/semiconductor interface" [Established].
- **Mobility and orientation (Mooney, ECS Interface, Spring 2005):** "The highest electron mobility is found using Si(100) substrates; however, the highest hole mobility is observed when devices are fabricated in Si(110) substrates. These mobilities are for the <110> direction for both wafer orientations"; pFET drive current on bulk Si(110) "increased by 45% compared to control devices fabricated on Si(100)"; hybrid-orientation ring oscillators "with gate delay reduced by 21%" [Established; reported research results].
- **Strain (same article):** electron mobility enhancement "a factor of 1.5-2.0 for strain in the range of 0.4-1.0%"; "hole mobility enhancement factors are lower than for electrons, 1.0-1.4 for tensile strain in the range 1.0-1.9%"; uniaxial tensile strain "by the deposition of stressed silicon nitride films", and "SiGe grown selectively in the source and drain regions" compressing the pFET channel; bonded strained-Si-on-insulator threading dislocation density "about 5 x 10⁵ cm⁻²" in the best case. The article's opening motivates mobility enhancement by the growing difficulty of reducing "critical dimensions such as the gate oxide thickness" [Established].
- **Intel strained silicon (Crossref records):** S. E. Thompson et al., "A 90-nm logic technology featuring strained-silicon", IEEE TED 51(11), 1790–1797, Nov 2004, DOI 10.1109/TED.2004.836648; K. Mistry et al., "Delaying forever: Uniaxial strained silicon transistors in a 90nm CMOS technology", VLSI Symposium 2004, pp. 50–51, DOI 10.1109/VLSIT.2004.1345387; K. Mistry et al., "A 45nm Logic Technology with High-k+Metal Gate Transistors, Strained Silicon, …", IEDM 2007, pp. 247–250, DOI 10.1109/IEDM.2007.4418914 [Established for the titles; contents not read].
- **Wafer size:** IRDS 2024 Executive Summary: "the 300 mm wafer size conversion saw the demise of the 'One Billion Club.' During this wafer size conversion, the cost of a new factory rapidly escalated from the hundreds of million to over 1 billion" [Established]. Infineon (11 Sep 2024): "Chip production on 300 mm wafers is technologically more advanced and significantly more efficient compared to 200 mm wafers, since the bigger wafer diameter offers 2.3 times more chips per wafer" [Established]. The G450C 450 mm program faded in the mid-2010s without a production fab (search excerpts from Cadence and Semiconductor Engineering only; end date about 2016–2017 uncertain) [Industry estimate].
- **Sources:** [Hu ch. 1–3](https://www.chu.berkeley.edu/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/Chenming-Hu_ch1-3.pdf); [Mooney 2005](https://www.electrochem.org/dl/interface/spr/spr05/IF03-05-Pg36-37.pdf); [Crossref record, Thompson 2004](https://api.crossref.org/works/10.1109/ted.2004.836648); [Crossref record, Mistry 2004](https://api.crossref.org/works/10.1109/vlsit.2004.1345387); [Crossref record, Mistry 2007](https://api.crossref.org/works/10.1109/IEDM.2007.4418914); [IRDS 2024 Executive Summary](https://irds.ieee.org/images/files/pdf/2024/2024IRDS_ES.pdf); [Infineon, 300 mm GaN](https://www.infineon.com/regional/austria/press-release/2024/gan300); 450 mm excerpts: [Cadence Breakfast Bytes](https://community.cadence.com/cadence_blogs_8/b/breakfast-bytes/posts/450mm-wafers), [Semiconductor Engineering](https://semiengineering.com/450mm-silicon-wafer-issues-emerge). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-163 · Carrier statistics, dopants and dopant fluctuation

*Read from the downloaded handbook copy and Hu's chapters on 19 Nov 2026 (Course 09 facts file F-0907, F-0908, F-0948); the n_i history is in R-086. Derived values are recomputed by `courses/09-semiconductor-devices/verify/verify_09_02.py`.*

- **Silicon, 300 K (Levinshtein & Rumyantsev, §1.1–1.2):** "Intrinsic carrier concentration (cm⁻³) 1 x 10¹⁰"; "Intrinsic resistivity (Ω x cm) 3.2 x 10⁵"; "Effective conduction band density of states 3.2 x 10¹⁹", valence 1.8 × 10¹⁹ cm⁻³; "Dielectric constant 11.7"; mobilities "electrons < 1400 … holes < 450" cm²/V·s [Established].
- **Shallow dopants in silicon (§1.2.5):** "Ionization energies of shallow donors (eV) As 0.054, P 0.045, Sb 0.043"; "shallow acceptors (eV) Al 0.072, B 0.045, Ga 0.074, In 0.157" [Established].
- **Band-gap narrowing (§1.2.3):** "For 10¹⁷ < N_d < 3 x 10²⁰ cm⁻³, ΔE_g ≈ 3.5 x 10⁻⁸ · N_d^(1/3) (eV)", read from a figure caption on electrical and optical gap narrowing (Van Overstraeten and Mertens 1987) [Established as a fit; other narrowing models differ by tens of meV at the highest doping, Contested].
- **Germanium and GaAs (§2.1, §4.1):** Ge n_i 2.0 × 10¹³ cm⁻³; GaAs n_i 2.1 × 10⁶ cm⁻³ and static dielectric constant 12.9 [Established].
- **Hu, ch. 1:** Table 1–4 N_c, N_v for Ge, Si and GaAs (R-161); "n_i at room temperature is roughly 10¹⁰ cm⁻³ for Si and 10⁷ cm⁻³ for GaAs"; Table 1–3 conductivity masses (Si electrons 0.26, holes 0.39 m0) [Established].
- **Hu, ch. 7, §7.7 and §7.8:** "Yet another example of random variation is the random dopant fluctuation phenomenon. The statistical variation of the number of dopant atoms and their location in small size MOSFET creates significant variations in the threshold voltage"; for ultra-thin-body MOSFETs, small depletion widths "can be obtained without heavy channel doping, carrier mobility is improved"; for multigate MOSFETs, "There is no need for heavy doping in the channel to reduce Wdep. This leads to low vertical field and less impurity scattering; as a result the mobility is higher" [Established].
- **Derived here:** n_i = √(N_C N_V) e^(−E_g/2kT) = 9.39 × 10⁹ cm⁻³ from the handbook N_C, N_V and E_g = 1.12 eV (2.7% below Altermatt's 9.65 × 10⁹); hydrogenic donor 25.8 meV and 2.38 nm (m* = 0.26, ε_r = 11.7); course n_i(T) model rising 528-fold from 300 to 400 K; dopant counts in example transistor volumes.
- **Sources:** M. E. Levinshtein, S. L. Rumyantsev and M. S. Shur (eds.), *Handbook Series on Semiconductor Parameters, Vol. 1* (World Scientific, 1996), library copy at [bdt.semi.ac.cn](https://bdt.semi.ac.cn/library/upload/files/2020/7/14133813301.pdf) (cite the book); [C. Hu, ch. 1–3](https://www.chu.berkeley.edu/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/Chenming-Hu_ch1-3.pdf); [C. Hu, ch. 7](https://www.chu.berkeley.edu/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/Chenming-Hu_ch7.pdf). Checked Nov 2026.

## R-164 · Carrier transport in silicon

*Read from the downloaded handbook copy (page images of §1.3 checked to confirm exponents) and Hu's chapter 2 on 19 Nov 2026 (Course 09 facts file F-0909, F-0910). Derived values are recomputed by `courses/09-semiconductor-devices/verify/verify_09_03.py`.*

- **Mobility against doping at 300 K (Levinshtein & Rumyantsev, eqs. 1.3.1 and 1.3.4, after Baccarani and Ostoja 1975):** μ ≈ μ_min + (μ_max − μ_min)/[1 + (N/N_ref)^α]; electrons μ_min = 92, μ_max = 1360 cm²/V·s, α = 0.91, N_ref = 1.3 × 10¹⁷ cm⁻³ (10¹⁵ ≤ N_d ≤ 10²⁰ cm⁻³); holes μ_min = 48, μ_max = 450 cm²/V·s, α = 0.76, N_ref = 6.3 × 10¹⁶ cm⁻³ [Established as fits].
- **Lattice mobility (eqs. 1.3.2 and 1.3.5, after Dorkel and Leturcq 1981):** electrons μ ≈ 1430 (300/T)^2.2 cm²/V·s for N_d ≤ 10¹⁵ cm⁻³ and 250–600 K; holes μ_L = 495 (300/T)^2.2 [Established].
- **Velocity against field (eqs. 1.3.6, 1.3.8, 1.3.9, after Jacoboni et al. 1977):** v_d ≈ v_s (F/F_c)/[1 + (F/F_c)^β]^(1/β); electrons (F ∥ ⟨111⟩, N_d ≤ 10¹⁶ cm⁻³, T > 250 K) v_s = 1.53 × 10⁹ T^−0.87 cm/s, F_c = 1.01 T^1.55 V/cm, β = 2.57 × 10⁻² T^0.66; holes (F ∥ ⟨100⟩) v_s ≈ 1.62 × 10⁸ T^−0.52 cm/s, F_c = 1.24 T^1.68 V/cm, β = 0.46 T^0.17 [Established].
- **Other silicon values (§1.1):** electron and hole thermal velocities 2.3 × 10⁵ and 1.65 × 10⁵ m/s; optical phonon energy 0.063 eV; diffusion coefficients ≤ 36 (electrons) and ≤ 12 cm²/s (holes); hole Hall factor about 0.75–0.9 at 300 K across 10¹⁴–10¹⁸ cm⁻³ (Fig. 1.3.9, Lin et al. 1981) [Established].
- **Hu, ch. 2:** Table 2–1, lightly doped mobilities at room temperature: Si 1400/470, Ge 3900/1900, GaAs 8500/400, InAs 30 000/500 cm²/V·s (electrons/holes); fits μ_p = 420/[1 + ((N_a + N_d)/1.6 × 10¹⁷)^0.7] + 50 and μ_n = 1318/[1 + ((N_a + N_d)/10¹⁷)^0.85] + 92 (Eqs. 2.2.8–2.2.9, read from the page image); "In small devices, the electric field can easily reach 10⁵ V/cm … electron and hole velocities saturate at around 10⁷ cm/s"; "Mobility and even velocity saturation … are concepts that … become fuzzy when we deal with devices whose sizes are smaller than the mean free path"; "it is not easy to measure mobility accurately and that we should presume the existence of uncertainties in experimental data in general" [Established].
- **Derived here:** the two fits agree within 6.8% for electrons over 10¹⁵–10¹⁹ cm⁻³ but differ by up to 55% for holes near 7 × 10¹⁷ cm⁻³ [Contested]; low-field mobility of the Jacoboni electron fit 1534 cm²/V·s at 300 K; D_n = 34.77 cm²/s at 10¹⁵ cm⁻³ by the Einstein relation.
- **Sources:** M. E. Levinshtein, S. L. Rumyantsev and M. S. Shur (eds.), *Handbook Series on Semiconductor Parameters, Vol. 1* (World Scientific, 1996), library copy at [bdt.semi.ac.cn](https://bdt.semi.ac.cn/library/upload/files/2020/7/14133813301.pdf) (cite the book); [C. Hu, ch. 2](https://www.chu.berkeley.edu/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/Chenming-Hu_ch2-2.pdf). Checked Nov 2026.
